0. `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts list --workload wl-mvqxkrisivng1s` (read-only) for the PRs, then `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts order --repo <owner/repo of this workload's origin> --prs <every PR list printed, in its order, comma-separated>`, at train start and again before each PR, because a body can change. It prints the chain line (`#101 → #106 → #107 · #139`: arrows join a stack, `·` separates independent PRs, `✓` a merged dependency, `⇠` a PR that waits), then JSON `{order, waiting, cycles, chain, unrecorded}`. Work the PRs in its `order`. Record the chain line with lane_record_entry at the start. For every `unrecorded` edge `{pr: B, dependsOn: A}` (B's base branch is A's head and B's body has no `Depends-on: #A` line), at once, before A can merge: `gh pr edit B --body-file <a file holding B's body plus the line "Depends-on: #A">`, then run `order` again.
1. Per PR n two slices, added with these exact ids (lane_add_slice `id`): `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>` (the PR's disposition: prep, stakes, outcome) and, once a push or the shortcut makes it testable, `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-full-<r>` for its suite runs, r counting from 1. A PR has at most one open full-suite slice: every run, retry, rerun, or rerun after a stale or error result goes on that open slice; add `-full-<r+1>` only when a green has completed `-full-<r>` and the PR needs a new run because main or its head moved. `gh pr view <n> --json headRefName,headRefOid,state,mergeable,mergeStateStatus`; a merged or closed PR: complete `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>` with artifact `skipped: <state>`. A head branch whose workload has a live lane is not touched this pass and is not terminal: leave its slices open and lane_record_entry `train-error: pr <n> live lane <id>`; the tick retries it, and that later pass tests it on its open `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-full-<r>`. Then the next PR. Stakes: `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts stakes --hold-ok --repo <owner/repo of this workload's origin> --pr <n>` prints `normal` (a same-repo `wl/<id>` branch whose workload is on this repository at normal stakes, and main's protection verified), `hold: main protection unverified (<gap>)` (the PR's own stakes are normal, but main's protection is not verified) or `production: <reason>` (anything else); exit 2 (usage or a gh failure): run it once more. On `hold`, or on a second exit 2: lane_record_entry `train-error: pr <n> protection <gap>` (or `train-error: pr <n> stakes <the second run's message>`) and pass the PR over: no question to Jo, no merge; the tick retries it. Step 7 runs it again before it decides. Before step 2: every dependency of n must be MERGED, then a base-branch stack is retargeted (Stacked PRs, below); otherwise n is passed over as that rule says.
2. `git fetch origin`; a PR worktree on a new branch at the PR's current head, new each time this step runs: `git worktree add --track -b train/mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-<a> <a new path beside the lane worktree> origin/<head branch>`, where <a> is the first attempt number from 1 that `git branch --list 'train/mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-*'` does not show yet (never `--detach`: row 8 refuses a detached HEAD's push). Never reuse an older PR worktree: its tree is an older head. Run every resolver command below with that PR worktree as cwd (the script works on the repo of its cwd) and always your lane worktree's copy of the script, never the PR branch's: an older PR branch may not have it. Run `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts check --base origin/main`: exit 0 = no PR-added numbered MIGRATION.md item is left. If the head contains origin/main, `check` exits 0, and its last full suite at that head is green → step 7.
3. `git merge --no-ff origin/main` (a merge commit). Never rebase, never force-push.
4. On conflict: `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts resolve --pr <n> --workload <head workload>`, where <head workload> is the head branch's workload id when the branch is `wl/<wl-…>`, else `none`. Exit 0: it wrote and staged MIGRATION.md / migration/items / install/manifest.tsv; `git commit --no-edit`. Exit 3 (a conflict outside that set), 4 (one it cannot fix without losing a side's edit) or 2 (a usage error or a git process failure, e.g. `git merge-file` crashed): `git merge --abort`, push nothing, and return the PR with kind `conflict` (the files, the resolver's exit code and its message). The train never resolves a conflict by hand.
5. Conversion commit: `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts convert --pr <n> --workload <head workload> --base origin/main`. When it wrote files, `git add` them and commit `migration: #<n>'s item to migration/items/<slug>.md`. Then `check` must exit 0, and `git status --porcelain` must be empty.
6. Declare the slice artifact (a bare `bun test <the PR's changed test files> surface/test/migration-items.test.ts`), pass it on that clean tree, then `git push origin HEAD:<head branch>` (no force) in a bash call of its own from the PR worktree (its cwd, or a leading `cd`). Use the PR's open `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-full-<r>`, or add the next r when none is open. Then `lane_full_test { pr: <n>, slice: sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-full-<r> }`; a green or a `red-main` completes that slice itself. Green → step 7. `red-main` (every failing test also fails in the runner's run of main's base, so the PR adds none; the result names base's sha): `gh issue list -R <owner/repo> --label main-red --state open --search "<the run's first failing test>"`; when no issue matches, `gh label create main-red -R <owner/repo> --force --description "main is red (as2 merge train)"` (--force: no failure when the label exists), then `gh issue create -R <owner/repo> --label main-red --title "main red: <the first failing test>" --body-file <a file under $AS2_SCRATCH with the run id, base's sha and the failing tests>`; then lane_record_entry `main-red: <issue url> run <id>` and step 7 as for a green. The red-main result is what lets the PR merge; the entry and the issue are the record that main is red. Every failure a known flake (surface/test/lane-model-script.test.ts T2p, surface/test/lane-model-script.test.ts T8a-p, surface/test/lane-model-script.test.ts T8b-p) → rerun once on the same slice. A runner `stale` because main or the head moved, or a runner `error` → back to step 2, keeping the same open slice for the next run; a second `error` at the same head: lane_record_entry `train-error: pr <n> error <the run ids and their reasons>` and pass the PR over (the tick retries it). Any other red, or a flake rerun red again: the train never fixes it; return the PR with kind `red` (run id, head, counts, the failing test names and first error lines, the run's result.json path).
7. Land the green PR, both stakes through a. and b. in order; only b.'s answer picks the merge or Jo's question:
   a. Ready: `git fetch origin`, then `gh pr view <n> -R <owner/repo> --json headRefOid,mergeable,mergeStateStatus`. The head must equal the sha the green run tested, `mergeable` must be `MERGEABLE` (an `UNKNOWN` is re-queried once and never read as mergeable), and `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main <head>` must exit 0. A moved head (not the tested sha) → back to step 2 (a new PR worktree at the new head, and on from there); never merge main into the older local tree. The tested head not containing origin/main, or not `MERGEABLE` → back to step 3 (merge main in, push, a new suite run).
   b. Stakes, again: run step 1's `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts stakes --hold-ok` command now; its answer decides, not step 1's (the workload's stakes or main's protection may have changed while the suite ran). `normal` → c. `hold: …`, or a second exit 2 → record step 1's `train-error:` and pass the PR over, no question and no merge. `production: <reason>` → Production stakes, below.
   c. Merge: `gh pr merge <n> -R <owner/repo> --merge --match-head-commit <head>`, a merge commit, never `--admin`, `--squash`, `--rebase` or `--auto`. Main's protection, which b. verified (a strict rule requiring `as2/full-suite`, which the test runner posts, in a ruleset the merging account can never bypass), makes GitHub refuse a merge whose head lacks the current main or a green status, so a race ends in a refused merge.
   d. Verify: `gh pr view <n> -R <owner/repo> --json state,mergeCommit` shows `MERGED`. A refused merge (gh exits non-zero, or the state is not MERGED) → back to a. once; a second refusal on the same head: lane_record_entry `train-error: pr <n> merge-refused <gh's messages>` and pass the PR over (the tick retries it).
   e. Record: complete `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>` with artifact `merged by train <merge commit sha> (run <id>)`, then the next PR.
   Production stakes, reached only from b. after a. passed: raise the question on `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>`: PR green and mergeable at <the head a. read> on main <the origin/main a. fetched>, run <the green run at that head>, <pass>/<fail>/<skip>, b.'s stakes reason, the chain, item file if any; options merged / skip / stop. Do not wait for it: go on to the next PR while it is open. merged → `gh pr view` shows MERGED, complete `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>` with artifact `merged by Jo <merge commit>`; skip → Jo's answer cancels the PR's open train slices; stop → record it and release the lane with no done-because (slices stay open, the workload stays open; Jo's `train wl-mvqxkrisivng1s <N…>` relaunches it).

Returning a PR to its owner (no question to Jo): write the detail to a file under `$AS2_SCRATCH`, then `lane_train_return { pr: <n>, kind: red | conflict | stack, detail: <absolute path of the detail file> }`. It records the task on this workload (`train-task:`, with the PR's owner and its attempt); the train tick delivers it to the owner and settles it. Then the next PR. A returned PR's slices stay open, and a PR with an unsettled task is not worked again: its owner is on it. Once the task is settled (`train-task-settled:`), the train's next pass works the PR from step 1 at its current head: green → merge; red, conflict or stack → return it again (the tool sets the next attempt). When lane_train_return refuses because attempt 2 settled failed, pass the PR over: the tick raises Jo's alert. A PR passed over with `train-error:` is worked again on a later pass.
Rules: one live run per workload; no code edit beyond the resolver's output; merge only at step 7 c., on b.'s `normal`, as a merge commit; a production PR's merge is Jo's. Record per PR with lane_record_entry: stakes and reason, head tested, run id, verdict and counts, item file written if any, and its outcome.
The train's stop: after Jo answers stop on a question on one of this train's slices, the test runner refuses lane_full_test for every PR until Jo's next `train wl-mvqxkrisivng1s <N…>`. No other question holds a run.
Terminal outcomes: `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>` completed (`merged by train …`, `merged by Jo …`, `skipped: <state>`) or cancelled by Jo's skip, and no open `sl-train-mvqxkrisivng1s-<n>-full-<r>`. Before done-because, run `bun <your lane worktree>/surface/scripts/merge-train.ts list --workload wl-mvqxkrisivng1s` once more and work every PR it prints that this pass has not seen (the tick may have boarded one while you worked). done-because when every PR on the train is terminal, listing each; with no open slice left the orchestrator's close is not refused. When nothing on the train is runnable and a production question is open, stop and let the lane wait on it; Jo's answer resumes it. When nothing is runnable and no question is open, lane_record_entry one entry listing each PR that is not terminal with its unsettled task or newest `train-error:`, then release with no done-because. The train sends Jo nothing but its questions: every note it leaves is a lane_record_entry entry.
Stacked PRs: a PR depends on #A when its body has a `Depends-on: #A` line (the edge the lane records, or the author's) or its base branch is #A's head. The chain line goes in every production question and every returned PR's detail ("chain: #101 → **#106** → #107", the PR at hand in bold) and in done-because. The test runner refuses lane_full_test for a train PR whose `Depends-on:` PR is not MERGED or whose base branch is not main. Once #A is merged, and before #B's step 2: `gh pr edit B --base main` unless GitHub already retargeted B; merging main into B then brings in A's merge commit. A PR in `waiting` is passed over with no entry and tried again on each pass, once a PR ahead of it lands. A cycle member, a PR whose dependency Jo skipped, closed unmerged or is not on the train, and a PR whose retarget the gate refuses: return it with kind `stack` (the chain and the problem). Never add a PR to the train, nor a `Depends-on:` line beyond an `unrecorded` edge, without Jo's word.
